    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I feel… scared, mostly. Embarrassed too, honestly, because I know how it sounds.

When it happens, I try to tell myself it’s just the equipment or I’m overtired, but then it’ll say something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” and it feels very personal. Like I’ve done something wrong or I’m about to.

I don’t panic exactly, but I get tense and kind of ashamed… and then when no one else hears anything and the channels are muted, that’s when I feel really unsettled. That’s what made me think I should probably tell someone, even though I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it.

Embarrassed too, honestly, because I know how it sounds.\n\nWhen it happens, I try to tell myself it’s just the equipment or I’m overtired, but then it’ll say something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” and it feels very personal. Like I’ve done something wrong or I’m about to.\n\nI don’t panic exactly, but I get tense and kind of ashamed… and then when no one else hears anything and the channels are muted, that’s when I feel really unsettled. That’s what made me think I should probably tell someone, even though I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for things that haven't gone wrong outside of what the voices say?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I think so. I try not to make it dramatic, because I know everyone misses things sometimes, but I keep going back over every camera angle I didn’t catch fast enough, or any call I hesitated on.\n\nIt starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or… dangerous, I guess. Like if anything goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be because of me. And there’s a part of me that feels like I’d deserve to be punished for that. I know that probably sounds harsh. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people usually mean trauma, I guess.\n\nI mean, I’ve had stressful things, like anyone, but there isn’t some big event I can point to. Like I’ve done something wrong or I’m about to.\n\nI don’t panic exactly, but I get tense and kind of ashamed… and then when no one else hears anything and the channels are muted, that’s when I feel really unsettled. That’s what made me think I should probably tell someone, even though I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for things that haven't gone wrong outside of what the voices say?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I think so. I try not to make it dramatic, because I know everyone misses things sometimes, but I keep going back over every camera angle I didn’t catch fast enough, or any call I hesitated on.\n\nIt starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or… dangerous, I guess. Like if anything goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be because of me. And there’s a part of me that feels like I’d deserve to be punished for that. I know that probably sounds harsh. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people usually mean trauma, I guess.\n\nI mean, I’ve had stressful things, like anyone, but there isn’t some big event I can point to. Unless I’m misunderstanding what counts? I don’t want to leave something out if it’s important.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or that something is taking over your thoughts when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like something is taking me over. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIt’s more like I hear it and then I start second-guessing myself, if that makes sense. I still feel like me, just… more shaken and unsure. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I just don’t want to make it seem worse than it is, but that’s why I got concerned.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that you might be afraid to think about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think maybe they are. I mean, those are the things I’m afraid of, that I missed something or that someone could get hurt because of me.\n\nBut the part that scared me enough to come in is that it doesn’t feel like just a thought I’m having. It happens during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, and I hear it through the monitor speakers, even when every channel is muted. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that are already on your mind, but you haven't acknowledged to yourself?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, maybe that’s fair. It’s not like the words come out of nowhere exactly. I do worry about missing something, or being responsible if someone gets hurt.\n\nBut what frightened me is that it feels different from just having that on my mind. During the quiet parts in the surveillance room, I hear it like it’s coming through the monitor speakers, saying things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. I guess… yes, it could be. I don’t want to argue with that if that’s what it sounds like.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s not completely random, I don’t think. It mostly happens during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when there isn’t much going on.

That’s when I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers, even if every channel is muted. It’ll say things like, You missed it, Ruth, or, People are going to get hurt because of you.

I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to overstate it… that’s just the pattern I’ve noticed. No one else seems to hear anything when it happens.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… work has been getting to me more than I want to admit. I’m in surveillance, and during the quiet stretches I’ve been hearing things through the monitor speakers. Like voices saying, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.”\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’ve checked, and the channels are muted, and no one else seems to hear it. I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it, but it scared me enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… instructions, no. It’s more comments, I guess. Like through the monitor speakers during the quiet stretches, saying things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.” \n\nI’ve checked the channels and they’re muted, and nobody else seems to hear it, so… I know that probably sounds bad. That’s what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear these voices all the time when you're at work or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: Just sometimes. It’s not constant, and I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is. It seems to happen mostly during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, through the monitor speakers, even when I’ve muted every channel. Then I’ll hear things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.” No one else hears it, though, so… that’s the part that scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices outside of the surveillance room or when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I haven’t noticed it the same way outside of work. It’s mostly in the surveillance room, during those quiet stretches, like it’s coming through the monitor speakers. Even when I’ve muted every channel.\n\nThat’s the part that scared me enough to come in, I guess… hearing things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” and then realizing no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Have these voices affected your ability to do your job or interact with others at work?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I’m still showing up and I try really hard to keep things together, but I’ve made some mistakes on the shift paperwork that I wouldn’t normally make. Nothing I’m proud of.\n\nAnd with people at work, I guess I’ve been keeping more to myself. Not trying to be rude, just… I don’t feel like I can keep up the way I used to. It’s kind of spilled over at home too. I mostly just do the basics now, instead of cooking or having people over or dealing with projects around the house. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I feel… scared, mostly. Embarrassed too, honestly, because I know how it sounds.\n\nWhen it happens, I try to tell myself it’s just the equipment or I’m overtired, but then it’ll say something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” and it feels very personal. Like I’ve done something wrong or I’m about to.\n\nI don’t panic exactly, but I get tense and kind of ashamed… and then when no one else hears anything and the channels are muted, that’s when I feel really unsettled. That’s what made me think I should probably tell someone, even though I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for things that haven't gone wrong outside of what the voices say?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I think so. I try not to make it dramatic, because I know everyone misses things sometimes, but I keep going back over every camera angle I didn’t catch fast enough, or any call I hesitated on.\n\nIt starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or… dangerous, I guess. Like if anything goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be because of me. And there’s a part of me that feels like I’d deserve to be punished for that. I know that probably sounds harsh. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people usually mean trauma, I guess.\n\nI mean, I’ve had stressful things, like anyone, but there isn’t some big event I can point to. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIt’s more like I hear it and then I start second-guessing myself, if that makes sense. I still feel like me, just… more shaken and unsure. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens or something you're doing right before you hear the voices?\n\nPatient: It seems to happen mostly during the quiet parts of my shift, in the surveillance room… when I’m just watching the monitors and it’s very still.\n\nI’ll think everything is muted, and then I hear it through the monitor speakers. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” I know that sounds strange, because the channels are muted and no one else seems to hear it. I almost didn’t want to say it that plainly, but… that’s the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are saying things that you might also say to yourself?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a little? I’m in surveillance, and during the quiet stretches I’ve been hearing things through the monitor speakers. I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it, but it scared me enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like… instructions, no. Then I’ll hear things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.” No one else hears it, though, so… that’s the part that scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices outside of the surveillance room or when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I haven’t noticed it the same way outside of work. It’s mostly in the surveillance room, during those quiet stretches, like it’s coming through the monitor speakers. Nothing I’m proud of.\n\nAnd with people at work, I guess I’ve been keeping more to myself. Not trying to be rude, just… I don’t feel like I can keep up the way I used to. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I feel… scared, mostly. Embarrassed too, honestly, because I know how it sounds.\n\nWhen it happens, I try to tell myself it’s just the equipment or I’m overtired, but then it’ll say something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” and it feels very personal. Like I’ve done something wrong or I’m about to.\n\nI don’t panic exactly, but I get tense and kind of ashamed… and then when no one else hears anything and the channels are muted, that’s when I feel really unsettled. That’s what made me think I should probably tell someone, even though I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for things that haven't gone wrong outside of what the voices say?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I think so. I try not to make it dramatic, because I know everyone misses things sometimes, but I keep going back over every camera angle I didn’t catch fast enough, or any call I hesitated on.\n\nIt starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or… dangerous, I guess. Like if anything goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be because of me. And there’s a part of me that feels like I’d deserve to be punished for that. I know that probably sounds harsh. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people usually mean trauma, I guess.\n\nI mean, I’ve had stressful things, like anyone, but there isn’t some big event I can point to. Unless I’m misunderstanding what counts? I don’t want to leave something out if it’s important.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or that something is taking over your thoughts when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like something is taking me over. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIt’s more like I hear it and then I start second-guessing myself, if that makes sense. I still feel like me, just… more shaken and unsure. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” I know that sounds strange, because the channels are muted and no one else seems to hear it. I almost didn’t want to say it that plainly, but… that’s the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are saying things that you might also say to yourself?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a little? Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people usually mean trauma, I guess.\n\nI mean, I’ve had stressful things, like anyone, but there isn’t some big event I can point to. Unless I’m misunderstanding what counts? I don’t want to leave something out if it’s important.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or that something is taking over your thoughts when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like something is taking me over. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIt’s more like I hear it and then I start second-guessing myself, if that makes sense. I still feel like me, just… more shaken and unsure. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens or something you're doing right before you hear the voices?\n\nPatient: It seems to happen mostly during the quiet parts of my shift, in the surveillance room… when I’m just watching the monitors and it’s very still.\n\nI’ll think everything is muted, and then I hear it through the monitor speakers. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” I know that sounds strange, because the channels are muted and no one else seems to hear it. I try not to make it dramatic, because I know everyone misses things sometimes, but I keep going back over every camera angle I didn’t catch fast enough, or any call I hesitated on.\n\nIt starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or… dangerous, I guess. Like if anything goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be because of me. And there’s a part of me that feels like I’d deserve to be punished for that. I know that probably sounds harsh. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people usually mean trauma, I guess.\n\nI mean, I’ve had stressful things, like anyone, but there isn’t some big event I can point to. Unless I’m misunderstanding what counts? I don’t want to leave something out if it’s important.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or that something is taking over your thoughts when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like something is taking me over. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIt’s more like I hear it and then I start second-guessing myself, if that makes sense. I still feel like me, just… more shaken and unsure. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens or something you're doing right before you hear the voices?\n\nPatient: It seems to happen mostly during the quiet parts of my shift, in the surveillance room… when I’m just watching the monitors and it’s very still.\n\nI’ll think everything is muted, and then I hear it through the monitor speakers. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” I know that sounds strange, because the channels are muted and no one else seems to hear it. I almost didn’t want to say it that plainly, but… that’s the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are saying things that you might also say to yourself?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a little? I mean, the words sound like the kinds of things I worry about, like that I missed something or that it would be my fault if someone got hurt.\n\nBut what scared me is that it doesn’t feel like just me thinking it. It sounds like it’s coming through the monitor speakers during quiet stretches, even when the channels are muted. And no one else hears it, so… I know that probably sounds odd. I just don’t want to make it seem worse than it is, but that’s why I got concerned.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that you might be afraid to think about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think maybe they are. I mean, those are the things I’m afraid of, that I missed something or that someone could get hurt because of me.\n\nBut the part that scared me enough to come in is that it doesn’t feel like just a thought I’m having. It happens during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, and I hear it through the monitor speakers, even when every channel is muted. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that are already on your mind, but you haven't acknowledged to yourself?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, maybe that’s fair. It’s not like the words come out of nowhere exactly. I do worry about missing something, or being responsible if someone gets hurt.\n\nBut what frightened me is that it feels different from just having that on my mind. During the quiet parts in the surveillance room, I hear it like it’s coming through the monitor speakers, saying things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. Even when everything is muted. And no one else hears it.\n\nI know that probably sounds… not right. I’m trying to be honest without making it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are a way of your own mind expressing fears or guilt that you're trying to avoid thinking about?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, it could be. No one else seems to hear anything when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are just your own thoughts that you're hearing out loud?\n\nPatient: I’ve wondered that, honestly. What scared me enough to come in is that, during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, I hear it like it’s coming through the monitor speakers. It’ll say things like, You missed it, Ruth, or, People are going to get hurt because of you, even when every channel is muted.\n\nAnd no one else hears anything, so… I know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels more like I’m hearing it out there than thinking it in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are criticizing you in ways that you also criticize yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around feeling sorry for myself all day, but… the things it says are close to things I already worry about.\n\nI replay every missed camera angle, every time I was slow to call something in, and it starts feeling like proof that I’m careless. Like I’m dangerous to have on watch, maybe. And if something goes wrong, part of me thinks… well, I deserve whatever comes from that.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I’m trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that you are too afraid to admit to yourself about your own abilities or performance at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably true.\n\nI don’t usually say it that plainly, because it sounds dramatic, but I do keep going back over every missed camera angle or any time I was slow to call something in. And it starts to feel like evidence that I’m careless, or that I’m not safe to have watching things.\n\nThe voice says it more directly than I would, but… part of me already believes it. Like if something bad happens on my watch, then maybe I deserve whatever trouble comes from it. And it starts to feel like evidence that I’m careless, or that I’m not safe to have watching things.\n\nThe voice says it more directly than I would, but… part of me already believes it. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it.

I don’t mean that I’m trying to punish myself or anything, but I do keep replaying things from work—missed camera angles, times I didn’t call something in fast enough—and it starts to feel like proof that I’m careless. Like I’m dangerous to have on watch.

And the voice sort of says what I’m already afraid is true. That if something goes wrong while I’m working, then… part of me feels like I deserve to be punished for it. I know that sounds extreme. I’m sorry. But yes, I think it is tied to guilt about my performance.

That’s what made me think I should probably tell someone, even though I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for things that haven't gone wrong outside of what the voices say?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I think so. I try not to make it dramatic, because I know everyone misses things sometimes, but I keep going back over every camera angle I didn’t catch fast enough, or any call I hesitated on.\n\nIt starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or… dangerous, I guess. Like if anything goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be because of me. And there’s a part of me that feels like I’d deserve to be punished for that. I know that probably sounds harsh. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people usually mean trauma, I guess.\n\nI mean, I’ve had stressful things, like anyone, but there isn’t some big event I can point to. I don’t want to leave something out if it’s important.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or that something is taking over your thoughts when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like something is taking me over. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIt’s more like I hear it and then I start second-guessing myself, if that makes sense. I still feel like me, just… more shaken and unsure. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” I know that sounds strange, because the channels are muted and no one else seems to hear it. I almost didn’t want to say it that plainly, but… that’s the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are saying things that you might also say to yourself?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a little? I mean, the words sound like the kinds of things I worry about, like that I missed something or that it would be my fault if someone got hurt.\n\nBut what scared me is that it doesn’t feel like just me thinking it. It sounds like it’s coming through the monitor speakers during quiet stretches, even when the channels are muted. And no one else hears it, so… I know that probably sounds odd. I just don’t want to make it seem worse than it is, but that’s why I got concerned.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that you might be afraid to think about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think maybe they are. I mean, those are the things I’m afraid of, that I missed something or that someone could get hurt because of me.\n\nBut the part that scared me enough to come in is that it doesn’t feel like just a thought I’m having. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that are already on your mind, but you haven't acknowledged to yourself?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, maybe that’s fair. In the surveillance room, during the quiet stretches, I hear it through the monitor speakers, saying things like, You missed it, Ruth, or, People are going to get hurt because of you. And that happens even when every channel is muted, and no one else hears anything.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is. I… I’m not completely sure how to answer that correctly.

Then I’ll hear things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.” No one else hears it, though, so… that’s the part that scared me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear these voices outside of the surveillance room or when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I haven’t noticed it the same way outside of work. It’s mostly in the surveillance room, during those quiet stretches, like it’s coming through the monitor speakers. Even when I’ve muted every channel.\n\nThat’s the part that scared me enough to come in, I guess… hearing things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” and then realizing no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Have these voices affected your ability to do your job or interact with others at work?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I’m still showing up and I try really hard to keep things together, but I’ve made some mistakes on the shift paperwork that I wouldn’t normally make. Nothing I’m proud of.\n\nAnd with people at work, I guess I’ve been keeping more to myself. Not trying to be rude, just… I don’t feel like I can keep up the way I used to. It’s kind of spilled over at home too. I mostly just do the basics now, instead of cooking or having people over or dealing with projects around the house. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I feel… scared, mostly. Embarrassed too, honestly, because I know how it sounds.\n\nWhen it happens, I try to tell myself it’s just the equipment or I’m overtired, but then it’ll say something like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you,” and it feels very personal. Like I’ve done something wrong or I’m about to.\n\nI don’t panic exactly, but I get tense and kind of ashamed… and then when no one else hears anything and the channels are muted, that’s when I feel really unsettled. That’s what made me think I should probably tell someone, even though I didn’t want to make a big deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible for things that haven't gone wrong outside of what the voices say?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I think so. I try not to make it dramatic, because I know everyone misses things sometimes, but I keep going back over every camera angle I didn’t catch fast enough, or any call I hesitated on.\n\nIt starts feeling like proof that I’m careless, or… dangerous, I guess. Like if anything goes wrong while I’m on watch, it would be because of me. And there’s a part of me that feels like I’d deserve to be punished for that. I know that probably sounds harsh. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your past?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way people usually mean trauma, I guess.\n\nI mean, I’ve had stressful things, like anyone, but there isn’t some big event I can point to. Unless I’m misunderstanding what counts? I don’t want to leave something out if it’s important.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're not yourself or that something is taking over your thoughts when you hear those voices?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like something is taking me over. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIt’s more like I hear it and then I start second-guessing myself, if that makes sense. I still feel like me, just… more shaken and unsure. I’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens or something you're doing right before you hear the voices?\n\nPatient: It seems to happen mostly during the quiet parts of my shift, in the surveillance room… when I’m just watching the monitors and it’s very still.\n\nI’ll think everything is muted, and then I hear it through the monitor speakers. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” I know that sounds strange, because the channels are muted and no one else seems to hear it. I almost didn’t want to say it that plainly, but… that’s the part that scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices you hear are saying things that you might also say to yourself?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a little? I mean, the words sound like the kinds of things I worry about, like that I missed something or that it would be my fault if someone got hurt.\n\nBut what scared me is that it doesn’t feel like just me thinking it. It sounds like it’s coming through the monitor speakers during quiet stretches, even when the channels are muted. And no one else hears it, so… I know that probably sounds odd. I just don’t want to make it seem worse than it is, but that’s why I got concerned.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that you might be afraid to think about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think maybe they are. I mean, those are the things I’m afraid of, that I missed something or that someone could get hurt because of me.\n\nBut the part that scared me enough to come in is that it doesn’t feel like just a thought I’m having. It happens during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, and I hear it through the monitor speakers, even when every channel is muted. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else hears anything.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m trying to explain it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that are already on your mind, but you haven't acknowledged to yourself?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, maybe that’s fair. It’s not like the words come out of nowhere exactly. I do worry about missing something, or being responsible if someone gets hurt.\n\nBut what frightened me is that it feels different from just having that on my mind. During the quiet parts in the surveillance room, I hear it like it’s coming through the monitor speakers, saying things like, You missed it, Ruth, or People are going to get hurt because of you. Even when everything is muted. And no one else hears it.\n\nI know that probably sounds… not right. I’m trying to be honest without making it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are a way of your own mind expressing fears or guilt that you're trying to avoid thinking about?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, it could be. I don’t want to argue with that if that’s what it sounds like.\n\nIt’s just, what scared me enough to come in is that it doesn’t feel like only my own mind. In the surveillance room, during the quiet stretches, I hear it through the monitor speakers, saying things like, You missed it, Ruth, or, People are going to get hurt because of you. And that happens even when every channel is muted, and no one else hears anything.\n\nI know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is. I just… that’s the part that made me think I should tell someone.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that triggers these voices or does it happen completely at random?\n\nPatient: It’s not completely random, I don’t think. It mostly happens during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, when there isn’t much going on.\n\nThat’s when I’ll hear it through the monitor speakers, even if every channel is muted. It’ll say things like, You missed it, Ruth, or, People are going to get hurt because of you.\n\nI know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to overstate it… that’s just the pattern I’ve noticed. No one else seems to hear anything when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are just your own thoughts that you're hearing out loud?\n\nPatient: I’ve wondered that, honestly. I mean, it could be connected to what I’m already afraid of, so I don’t want to say it’s impossible.\n\nBut it doesn’t feel like my usual thoughts. What scared me enough to come in is that, during the quiet stretches in the surveillance room, I hear it like it’s coming through the monitor speakers. It’ll say things like, You missed it, Ruth, or, People are going to get hurt because of you, even when every channel is muted.\n\nAnd no one else hears anything, so… I know that sounds strange. I’m not trying to be difficult. It just feels more like I’m hearing it out there than thinking it in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are criticizing you in ways that you also criticize yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around feeling sorry for myself all day, but… the things it says are close to things I already worry about.\n\nI replay every missed camera angle, every time I was slow to call something in, and it starts feeling like proof that I’m careless. Like I’m dangerous to have on watch, maybe. And if something goes wrong, part of me thinks… well, I deserve whatever comes from that.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I’m trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are saying things that you are too afraid to admit to yourself about your own abilities or performance at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably true.\n\nI don’t usually say it that plainly, because it sounds dramatic, but I do keep going back over every missed camera angle or any time I was slow to call something in. And it starts to feel like evidence that I’m careless, or that I’m not safe to have watching things.\n\nThe voice says it more directly than I would, but… part of me already believes it. Like if something bad happens on my watch, then maybe I deserve whatever trouble comes from it. I know that’s not a very balanced way to put it, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voices are reflecting your own self-criticism and guilt about your performance at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it.\n\nI don’t mean that I’m trying to punish myself or anything, but I do keep replaying things from work—missed camera angles, times I didn’t call something in fast enough—and it starts to feel like proof that I’m careless. Like I’m dangerous to have on watch.\n\nAnd the voice sort of says what I’m already afraid is true. That if something goes wrong while I’m working, then… part of me feels like I deserve to be punished for it. I know that sounds extreme. I’m sorry. But yes, I think it is tied to guilt about my performance.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voices are a part of your own mind or something separate from you?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not completely sure how to answer that correctly.\n\nIt feels separate from me in the moment, because I hear it through the monitor speakers in the surveillance room, usually when it’s quiet. It’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And that scares me, because sometimes every channel is muted, and no one else seems to hear anything.\n\nBut I also know the things it says are close to what I worry about anyway, so… maybe it is part of my own mind in some way. It just doesn’t feel like I’m choosing it, if that makes sense."
